## Critical Constraints
- C1 — The note never invents a clinical sentence; nothing unconfirmed reaches a saved note (the only promotion in this plan is the clinician's Add through `add_line`); every transcript line is one contiguous interval rebuilt byte for byte by Check 1. Model text is never displayed, stored, copied or written.
- C2 — Check 5, the refusal filter and the ownership rule are ENFORCING controls: narrowing is routine; widening (the lexicon's content, the `patient` pin under the frame, any connective) is the practitioner's dated decision recorded in this plan; a change to the shipped list bumps `PROMPT_VERSION`. A miss on a measurement is prompt re-tuning or a finding, never a gate change.
- C3 — No persisted field, literal or schema bump in any encrypted record, no new key in `practitioner_settings.json`, and no new file at rest (D3): 0.3.5 reads every 0.3.6 note, audit row and settings file.
- C4 — ONE canonicalisation (`note_check.canonicalise`, which also yields the prompt's matches) under ONE tokenisation (`note.content_tokens`); the harness measures the register through Check 5's own verdicts (`ProseCounts`), never a second fold.
- C5 — No clinical content in a tracked file; the agent never reads, plays or describes a real transcript, note or recording (development-recordings C1); the harness runs synthetic sets only; the register sheet reads fixture notes only; every report is counts, ids and codes.
- C6 — The rule in force is never edited after a run; a metric change is a dated amendment before the run (one here: D10's columns); predictions before any run of record; `heldout-6` is read by nobody but its author until P.2; `heldout-5` is spent; nothing under `desktop/src/` or `scripts/` changes between predictions v6 and run 9 except F.2's plumbing, which the predictions name — so the documents, the version strings and hardening H1–H4 ALL precede F.1 (codex PR-MED-007); a behaviour-changing fix found after F.1 resets the freeze (new predictions, and a new blind set if the old one was read by anyone).
- C7 — The transcript reaches the model ONLY through the suggestion prompt (D7), from one module and one call site (pinned by AST), for one call per window, with the runtime state cleared after it; the prose stage keeps seeing confirmed lines only; zero network (the no-sockets legs are the enforcing control, as for the prose runtime); the suggestion input's text field is a registered log-tripwire name.
- C8 — Every new text path, list, combo box, text widget or placement caller is a `test_shadow_exits.py` pin change; this plan adds none (the chooser is an existing `NoCopyListWidget`); a shadow recording's note never leaves the app and its Save teaches nothing.
- C9 — All work on the Windows host; one `/execute-loop` at a time on `main`; commit only on the practitioner's word; never push unless told; the ui-refresh branch is not touched; a plan edit asserts `"\x00" not in text` and no bare `\r`.
- C10 — Every settings file and models-root read goes through the conftest's pinned seams (`conftest.py:263-289` the models root; `:294-322` the settings roots); no test depends on the host's real files; every enablement assertion injects its assumption both ways.
- C11 — The shipping gate (rubric v1, the ten shadow consultations under `clean`), the clinician review guide's promises and the practice documents keep their meaning; a changed promise is a new dated line, never a quiet edit.
- C12 — The documents phase gets the cross-family peer pass; a plan or doc claim of what a build keeps, changes or refuses is checked against the code before that pass.
- C13 — A reviewed lifecycle is refactored in its own commit with its tests green BEFORE it is extended (C.2 before C.4); the custody shape the code implements for a review-stage job — abort at exit, then the orphan finishes its unit of work with the key already destroyed (`main_window.py:2066-2078`; retention row 52) — covers the suggestion job's transcript text exactly as it covers the prose job's note text: one window at most, named as a lifetime, never a reason to hold Complete or Discard.

- **MUST-PAUSE (Task 0.1, practitioner ratification), 2026-11-08T09:40+11:00 — CLOSED 2026-11-08T09:50+11:00: decided (a), the practitioner's words "0.1: (a) the drafted list as is, go with recommended" (on Task 0.1).** The brief as put: options (a) the drafted list as is; (b) the drafted list with the practitioner's additions / removals, each named; rec=executor:(a), once the composer's audit run exits 0 — every entry is a shipped abbreviation with its one standard expansion, a lay and a clinical name for the same body part, or a week count, and every abbreviation that could read as an everyday word or has two meanings is left out. **In plain English:** this list is the only shorthand the two prose writing styles (not Clean clinical) will be allowed to use. When a line says "lower back pain", the prose may write "LBP"; "three weeks" may become "3/52"; "kneecap" may become "patella" — and the safety check treats the two forms as the same words, so nothing else gets through. It holds 93 entries: 49 body parts (41 of your shipped abbreviations such as ACL, SIJ, TMJ, plus 8 everyday names such as kneecap → patella and shoulder blade → scapula), 4 complaint words (LBP, PGP, C/O "complains of", and pins and needles → paraesthesia), 27 examination terms (ROM, SLR, VAS, NWB and the like), "both sides" / bilateral → B/L, and the twelve week counts 1/52 to 12/52 (days and months stay as spoken, as decided). Of your 168 shipped abbreviations, 73 are used; the other 95 are left out: 30 because they are also everyday words and a check could mistake one for the other ("AS" is "as", "US" is "us", and also SOB, MET, Post, Traps, Add, Ant, ART, Flex, Glut, Hams, Mob, NAGS, PA, PROM, Quads, Rot, SNAGS, Sup, TA, TENS, WAD — plus seven I found: Ev "EV", HEP "hep B", HR "hr" for hour, Lat "lat" the gym muscle, Med "med", OT overtime, TP); 11 because they have two common meanings (Abd, Bx, Ext, IR, LSJ, LT, UT, PF plantarflexion or pelvic floor, Px, STM massage or mobilisation, Sx symptoms or surgery); 4 because their expansion contains "no", "on" or "within", which the check guards separately (NAD, NKDA, O/E, WNL); L and R (sides); ER (the check ignores "er" as a filler); Dist and Prox (they would clash with DIPJ and PIPJ); DOMS ("soreness" is on the never-swap list); and 44 treatment, diagnosis, history, test and referral abbreviations (HVLA, OA, Hx, MRI, GP and the like), because the design's five kinds of entry (body part, complaint, examination term, side, weeks) have no place for them — adding a sixth kind would change the locked design (D1) and would be a separate dated decision, not part of this list. Feelings words (sore, ache, tight, stiff, dull, sharp, numb, swelling, and 15 more such as tingling, burning, swollen) are on a never-swap list, so "sore" can never become "pain" and "swelling" never "effusion". To choose (b), name each entry to add or remove; an addition must be a by-definition equivalent (an abbreviation and its expansion, or a lay and a clinical name for the same thing).

- **MUST-PAUSE (Task C.0 `[decision]` — the suggestion window's size and whether placed neighbouring lines go in as context), 2026-11-09T04:44:25+11:00 — CLOSED: DECIDED (a) by the practitioner, "continue, go with recommended" (2026-11-09T04:38+11:00, given before these options were shown — the circumstance on Task C.0); recorded on Task C.0 and as D9's dated note by leg x2.** The derivation is the dated sub-bullet on Task C.0 (every input an estimate, named: the suggestion instruction is not written yet, the segment rate is the Phase 2 benchmark's, the share of left-out lines is assumed). Options: (a) `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 40`, context lines on — a window ≈ 36 s; a 25-minute consultation needs ≈ 315 s, so the 240 s cap stops it after 7 windows (≈ 250 s) with its last ≈ 5 minutes not assessed; ≤ 20 minutes assessed whole; (b) `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 100`, context lines on — a window ≈ 74 s; 25 minutes assessed whole in ≈ 265 s (≤ 28 minutes); (c) context lines off, `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 25` asked lines — a window ≈ 36 s; 25 minutes assessed whole in ≈ 160 s, short answers routed without their question. rec=executor:(a) — the 30–45 s target is met, the cap cuts only the end of a long consultation (mostly your own already-warned lines), and the context is what routes a short answer; (c) is the fair alternative if every line being looked at matters more. **In plain English:** after a consultation the app can ask the local model where each line it left out of the note belongs, and mark its guess beside that line in the "Add a line" list — you still choose and press Add. The model reads the transcript in chunks; this decides the chunk size, and whether each chunk also shows the lines around the left-out ones (with the section they already went to) so a short answer like "yeah, about three weeks" can be matched to its question. All the guesses appear at once when the model finishes. With (a) that is about 4 minutes after review starts for a 25-minute consultation (about 2 minutes for a 10-minute one); the last few minutes of a long consultation are not looked at — they stay as today, left out with their warning, and the count line says how many. With (b) everything is looked at in about 4½ minutes, but each chunk takes about 74 seconds. With (c) everything is looked at in about 2¾ minutes, but each left-out line is judged on its own, without the lines around it, so short answers are guessed less well. Complete and Discard never wait under any option (they stop the model's work at once). What does wait is at most one chunk: closing the app, opening another recording from the Recovery tab, or Save after you add a line in a prose writing style — about 36 seconds under (a) or (c), about 74 under (b); for that same time the transcript text stays in the computer's memory after Complete or Discard. These are estimates from the laptop's measured speed, which varied by about a third between evenings; the real numbers are measured later (C.6, and the live smoke).

Recorded by stage-3 leg x6 at 2026-11-09T16:54:13+11:00: Q1 (b) amends the locked D8 by the practitioner's decision (D8's dated amendment line) and is BUILT in this leg (`ui/note.py` `NoteScreen._stop_suggestions`, called by `save` and `abandon`; Round 19's SEC-004 / SEC-005 dispositions); Q2 (a) keeps the prose prompt guard deferred with its corrected record (the Deferred item's dated decision line; SEC-002's disposition). As put (kept for the record):
  - **Q1 — the suggestion job after Save, and before Delete note and complete (SEC-004, SEC-005; the locked D8).** What was found: Save leaves a running suggestion job running — up to its 240 s cap — though Save closes the Line chooser and Add, so no suggestion it makes can be used; it holds the consultation's transcript lines in memory meanwhile, and at Complete or Discard the session key is destroyed before the job is told to stop, so it can even start one more window after the key is gone. Delete note and complete — the one exit that can come before Save — likewise destroys the key first. The threat model's residue (s1) names only Complete and Discard. Options: **(a) Record it** — (s1), flow 29 and the retention row name Delete note and complete beside Complete and Discard, and say a live job runs on after Save until an exit; no code change. **(b) Stop the job at Save and before Delete note and complete (Recommended)** — `NoteScreen.save` (after a successful save) and the start of `NoteScreen.abandon` stop and orphan the suggestion job exactly as `prepare_close` does (the status line says no suggestions will come); a dated D8 amendment; the job then holds the transcript at most one window past Save, and Delete note and complete stops it before the key goes, so (s1) shrinks to "the window in flight at Save or at Delete note and complete"; the cost: for at most that one window after Save, closing the app or opening another recording's review waits (the close already waits so today); `test_ui_suggestions.py` tests change; nothing re-measured (the harness has no Save). **(c) Stop it at Save only** — as (b) without the Delete-note change; (s1) keeps naming Delete note and complete. `rec=executor:(b)` — after Save the job serves nothing, so (b) only shortens how long the transcript stays in memory, and closes the window-after-the-key case that (s1) does not name.
  - **Q2 — the prose prompt's guard (SEC-002; the deferral of 2026-11-08 made on a wrong reason).** What was found: the plan deferred "`_prompt_safe` on the prose prompt builder" (your "Defer (Recommended)", 2026-11-08) because "a confirmed line holding a `<|…|>` run is already refused as an echoed marker on the way out" — that is not so: the prose parser refuses only its own two list markers. A confirmed line, a learned shorthand token or a past-note example that spells a chat-template marker such as `<|im_end|>` would reach the prose prompt as a live control token. What still bounds it: Check 5 judges the prose against the confirmed lines themselves (never the prompt), and the recogniser cannot write `<|`, so the source would be something typed or pasted by you; the clinician reads every prose section before Save. Recorded now: the threat model's surface 17 and the deferral's corrected line. Options: **(a) Keep it deferred, with the corrected record (Recommended)** — built after run 9 as its own task (a shared prompt-safe step on the prose prompt's lines and conditioning, never on Check 5's inputs), with a new prompt version and its re-measurement. **(b) Build it now, before F.1** — the same change now: `PROMPT_VERSION` `narrative-v8`, R.5's paired fixture timing and R.6's run of the 50 under `--prose` repeated before the predictions. `rec=executor:(a)` — Check 5's gate holds whatever the prompt says, no spoken word can produce the marker, and (b) re-opens Phase R's measured prompt just before the freeze for a case that needs your own typing.

Phase P's pass should then hold a documents slice with one evidence entry per document: the plan's P.2 record and the harness's run 9 entry checked line by line against `.cursor/loops/run9/*.md`. A docs-only slice has found over-claims before, so it should not be skipped at the close.
    - (4) No prompt is written this leg.
  - **Gates:** `loop-history-check.py` clean. No ruff, mypy or pytest owed, since no package or test file changed.
  - **Owed to the composer:**
    1. Optionally, the document pins through the runner: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/lint-held-out-set.py validation/heldout-6 < /dev/null` (expected exit 0, 10589 passed, 9 skipped). The harness document changed, but not its tables. A targeted pytest is refused while the set is blind.
    2. The practitioner's P.4 words.
    3. The practitioner's word on committing the plan and `docs/testing/validation-harness.md`.
    4. Then P.5's build leg.

## Review Findings Log
### Round 1 — 2026-11-08
Source: Codex — codex gpt-6-astra high — plan peer-review

**PR-HIGH-001 — The register’s ordinary-word collision rule remains incomplete.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:164` principally excludes function words, but `clinical_abbreviations.json:144` includes `SOB`, whose folded form also means a crying sound; other omissions include `MET` (`:87`), `Post` (`:119`) and `Traps` (`:156`). A symptom entry for SOB and its clinical expansion satisfies the specified invariants, yet symmetric folding would permit the ordinary word to acquire the clinical meaning. This is a newly admitted meaning change, not merely the existing attachment residue.  
Proposed amendment: Require an ordinary-word collision audit of every proposed term before ratification, extending the pinned exclusions and regression cases beyond function words.

**PR-MED-001 — Rendering reuse does not bind the new frame eligibility.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:135` retains text-only binding. Editing the sole patient transcript line without changing its wording creates a clinician-provenance assertion without a speaker (`ui/note.py:1584`, `:1660`), making `other_voice` false while retaining the same digest (`note.py:1084`). `ui/models.py:4137` consequently carries the previously passed patient-framed rendering; arriving results have the same problem at `:4129`.  
Proposed amendment: Revalidate carried and arriving renderings against current frame eligibility, with same-text replacement and Undo tests, without adding a persisted field.

**PR-MED-002 — The proposed busy flag does not guard the cited Chrome or destructive paths.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: D8/C.4 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:171`, `:243`) extend `NoteScreen.is_busy`, but `ChromeBridge._screen` is a `SessionScreen` (`ui/bridge.py:261`, `:274`), and the cited guards read that screen (`:953`, `:1017`, `:1091`). There is also no Chrome Complete command (`protocol.py:148`). Desktop completion already destroys the key before `note_screen.clear()` (`ui/main_window.py:2070`, `:2075`), contradicting C13 while agreeing with the plan’s post-key plaintext residue at line 141.  
Proposed amendment: Define and wire the exit policy at the actual desktop and Chrome entry points, reconcile C13 with the accepted plaintext lifetime, and replace the nonexistent panel-Complete smoke with tests through real window/bridge wiring.

**PR-MED-003 — A whole-job busy flag cannot provide the promised one-window wait.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: Flow 2 and C.0 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:158`, `:239`) describe progress after the current window returns, but C.1 runs multiple windows inside one job (`:240`), and `ui/note.py:2828` remains busy until the job and its orphans finish. Even after correcting the guard wiring, the wait covers the whole stage, including yield waits and the final window beyond the cap check.  
Proposed amendment: Specify either cancellation of remaining windows for a pending exit or an explicitly accepted whole-stage wait, and verify the chosen behavior with a multi-window job.

**PR-MED-004 — The specified clock cannot exclude model-lock waits.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: D9/C.1 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:172`, `:240`) exclude lock waits from the cap and “Model seconds,” but the interface returns only text or token counts (`language_model.py:115`), acquiring its private lock inside both calls (`:327`, `:353`). An external clock cannot distinguish waiting from model work, and no task adds that capability.  
Proposed amendment: Add a tested admission/timing seam that separates lock acquisition from measured work before implementing the cap and reporting its duration.

**PR-MED-005 — R.1 needs an executable import-time validation arrangement.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:230` places every semantic invariant in the `note_config` loader, but `note_check.py:273` already imports `note_config`; the required laterality, medication and polarity definitions appear afterward at `:316`, `:347` and `:1762`. Importing them back during configuration loading creates a cycle.  
Proposed amendment: Specify structural validation in `note_config` and semantic validation after the canonical classifiers exist, with tests covering both import entry points.

**PR-MED-006 — The headline first-person transformation cannot pass the specified gate.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: The Goal’s example (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:26`) drops first-person tokens that remain mandatory under `note_check.py:1843`; those tokens are absent from `prose_connectives.json`, and its interrupted anatomy phrase cannot match the proposed contiguous phrase fold. R.5 (`plan:234`) requires patient-spoken fixtures without requiring this representative construction.  
Proposed amendment: Add realistic first-person/contraction fixtures and state the achievable output under the fixed gate, requiring a separate decision before any widening needed to deliver the headline example.

**PR-MED-007 — Mandatory later tasks violate the prediction freeze.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: C6 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:191`) forbids source changes after predictions except C.7a plumbing; C.7 commits those predictions at `:246`, but D.2 subsequently changes `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/__init__.py` (`:251`), and H1/H3 explicitly permit fixes afterward (`:255`, `:257`).  
Proposed amendment: Move versioning and behavior-changing hardening before the prediction freeze, or specify narrowly permitted changes and a mandatory prediction/blind-protocol reset when later fixes affect behavior.

**PR-MED-008 — Historical rule-v5 tests must not be moved to heldout-6.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:128` directs moving marked-R1 pins to `heldout-6`, repeated by C.7a (`:247`), but `test_validation_metrics.py:3946` and `:4094` explicitly load immutable rule-v5, whose assertions correctly name `heldout-5` (`:3958`, `:4105`). The parametrization at `:4150` compares historical v4/v5, and `test_validation_harness.py:1463` likewise checks v5’s refusal vocabulary.  
Proposed amendment: Preserve historical v4/v5 tests and add separate v6 cases, refusal expectations and equivalence assertions.

**PR-LOW-001 — R.2 requires incorrect first-failure codes.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:231` requires `protected` for a changed duration and rule (c) for a negation substitution, but unchanged rule ordering returns `missing_fact` first when the original token disappears (`note_check.py:1843`), before reaching polarity or protected-count checks (`:1849`, `:1851`).  
Proposed amendment: Assert the actual first failure for substitutions and use cases differing only in multiplicity to exercise rules (c) and (d).

**PR-LOW-002 — The scoring-sheet test targets the wrong directory.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: R.7 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:236`) adds the new sheet to `test_pilot_docs.py:195`, but that list checks files beneath `docs/pilot` (`:196`); the sheet belongs beneath `docs/testing`.  
Proposed amendment: Add a separate assertion against the sheet’s actual `docs/testing` path.

**PR-LOW-003 — The fact-preservation “tautology” rationale is false.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:97`, `:142` and `:168` claim every folded input token survives a passing verdict. Rule (a) explicitly permits dropping connectives and compares sets (`note_check.py:1838`), whereas harness canonicalization performs order-sensitive rewrites, including adjacent cardinal joins (`validation.py:1593`, `:1618`). Passing the gate therefore does not establish the claimed implication.  
Proposed amendment: Retain the fixed decision against another metric, but accurately describe token-set coverage and identify the practitioner sheet as the semantic check.

**PR-LOW-004 — The PT example claims a refusal that the gate cannot produce.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:144` says repurposing an existing PT token into patient framing fails rule (a), but the folded `pt` remains present, the reporting verb is already a connective, and the polarity/protected counts remain unchanged (`note_check.py:1836`).  
Proposed amendment: Describe this as a token-meaning ambiguity within the named residue rather than a guaranteed refusal.

**PR-LOW-005 — The chooser marks the boundary; it does not enforce it.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:132` and `:169` attribute boundary enforcement to `eligible_utterances`, but its boundary argument only adds a mark (`ui/models.py:2945`); `allowed_sections` does not consult it (`:2937`). The plan’s separate pre-boundary input cut supplies the actual control.  
Proposed amendment: Attribute boundary enforcement to request construction and describe the chooser as enforcing ownership/not-in-note while marking boundary lines.

**PR-LOW-006 — C.6 retains a stale seen-set count.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:204` and `:245` require five seen sets, although the run-8 record enumerates six including the 50 (`docs/testing/validation-harness.md:287`); Flow 3 already intends the 50 plus the seen held-out sets (`plan:161`).  
Proposed amendment: Enumerate `scripts`, `heldout`, and `heldout-2` through `heldout-5` explicitly in C.6 and its verification requirements.

Materiality: 11 build-affecting / 4 record-only / 0 invalid

**Composer disposition (2026-11-08, Fable 5.1; each finding verified against the cited code before acting):** PR-HIGH-001 applied (D1: the ordinary-word refusal set with the six found abbreviations; the fold expands to canonical token sets; Task 0.1's audit). PR-MED-001 applied (`section_input_digest` folds `other_voice`; R.3 tests). PR-MED-002 and PR-MED-003 applied together (the exit policy: a live job never busy, every exit aborts, only the in-flight window counts; C13 reworded; the Chrome-panel smoke replaced by the desktop one; Flow 2, D8, Integration Notes, P.3, C.4). PR-MED-004 applied (lock waits included; D9, C.1). PR-MED-005 applied (structural invariants in `note_config`, semantic in `note_check`; D1, R.1). PR-MED-006 applied as far as the plan may: the Goal's example restated as achievable, the set-expansion fold, first-person fixtures in R.5; the gate widening (first person as glue under the frame) is a `[decision]` for the practitioner — Task R.0. PR-MED-007 applied (Phase D and H1–H4 before the freeze; the freeze is Phase F; C6). PR-MED-008 applied (v5 tests untouched; v6 cases of their own). PR-LOW-001, PR-LOW-002 applied (R.2's first-failure codes; R.7's `docs/testing` assertion). PR-LOW-003, PR-LOW-004, PR-LOW-005, PR-LOW-006 applied as wording (token-set coverage; the `PT` residue; the chooser marks the boundary; six seen sets).

### Round 2 — 2026-11-08
Source: Codex — codex gpt-6-astra high — plan peer-review

**PR-HIGH-002 — Direct window close bypasses the amended exit policy.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:176` and `:330` exclude live suggestions from `is_busy` and put abort/orphaning in `clear()`. However, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:961` explicitly says accepted window close does not call `clear()`. After Save, the existing review guard is false (`:2828`), allowing the close guard at `ui/main_window.py:1478` to pass while the suggestion thread runs; `ui/note.py:2824` identifies destruction of a running QThread as a process-abort hazard.  
Proposed amendment: Add an explicit close-request cancellation/orphan transition before close eligibility is checked, retain the window until the worker finishes, and test closing directly after Save during both inference and a yield wait.

**PR-MED-009 — The digest amendment omits the shared producer/consumer contract.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:139`, `:171` and `:319` instruct changing `section_input_digest`, but its signature accepts only a section (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1084`), while frame eligibility also needs the note’s confirmed clinician. The shared stamping contract is `input_texts_digest` (`:1075`); changing only the section-side calculation would make freshly produced renderings fail `bound_rendering`’s comparison (`:1103`), including results handled through `ui/models.py:4129`.  
Proposed amendment: Specify one flag-aware digest used by rendering production and every binding/reuse path, thread the necessary note context through its callers, and test arriving results, carried results and the harness stage.

**PR-MED-010 — Duration data contradicts the canonical expansion.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: D1 fixes the first duration phrase as `three weeks` (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:169`), while D2 defines canonical output as the first phrase’s tokens but requires `{3, weeks}` (`:170`), repeated in the edge inventory (`:149`). `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:259` preserves `three`; no number-word conversion is specified.  
Proposed amendment: Put the numeric phrase first in each duration entry and pin exact expanded sequences, updating `:141` to distinguish the raw abbreviation’s date classification from the expanded numeral used by rule (d).

**PR-MED-011 — R.0’s recommended swap also removes modality.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:316` and `:170` make `i'd` and `i'll` droppable while promising that the frame replaces “the first person and nothing else.” Those contractions also carry conditional/future meaning; current tokenisation preserves them (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:249`), and `note_check.py:1766` explicitly protects modal meaning against conversion into recorded fact. This consequence is not disclosed by the proposed decision.  
Proposed amendment: Preserve modal-bearing contractions or their modal component, describe that behavior accurately in R.0, and pin it without deciding the practitioner’s option.

**PR-LOW-007 — Normative Chrome-busy wording survives.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:115` and D8’s heading at `:176` still require “the Chrome `busy` effect named and smoked,” contradicting D8’s amended body; the bridge reads `SessionScreen` (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/bridge.py:261`, `:274`), and `protocol.py:148` has no Complete command.  
Proposed amendment: Replace both normative statements with references to D8’s actual exit policy, leaving explicitly historical descriptions identified as history.

**PR-LOW-008 — Negation first-failure requirements remain inconsistent.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:149` still says the negation-substitution example “fails (c).” R.2 at `:318` conditions that outcome on differing counts, but differing counts alone do not bypass rules (a) and (b): disappearance of the original negation returns `missing_fact` at `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:1843`, before the counter comparison at `:1849`.  
Proposed amendment: Require `missing_fact` for the substitution and reserve the polarity test for unchanged token sets whose negation multiplicities differ, correcting both sibling statements.

**PR-LOW-009 — The scoring-sheet directory correction missed Key Findings.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:133` still says R.7’s sheet “joins that list,” although corrected R.7 at `:323` requires a separate `docs/testing` assertion; `desktop/tests/test_pilot_docs.py:195` checks the existing list under `PILOT`.  
Proposed amendment: Replace the Key Findings instruction with a reference to R.7’s separate assertion against the actual sheet path.

**PR-LOW-010 — D5 retains the rejected tautology claim.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:173` still describes the omitted metric as “a count that is 0 by construction,” despite the corrected rationale at `:101` and `:147`; the gate compares token sets with droppable connectives (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:1836`), while harness canonicalisation is order-sensitive (`validation.py:1593`).  
Proposed amendment: Replace the remaining construction claim with the corrected token-set limitation and practitioner-sheet rationale, preserving the fixed decision against another metric.

**PR-LOW-011 — Excluded scope still attributes boundary enforcement to the chooser.**  
Materiality: record-only  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:103` says `eligible_utterances` applies “the boundary,” whereas corrected D6 at `:174` assigns enforcement to the input cut; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:2937` computes allowed sections without the boundary, which only supplies a mark at `:2945`.  
Proposed amendment: Make the Excluded entry reference D6’s ownership checks and D7’s input cut instead of attributing boundary enforcement to the chooser.

PR-HIGH-001: confirmed — D1 (`plan:169`), Task 0.1 (`:311`) and R.1 (`:317`) require auditing every proposed term and regression cases. Additional ordinary-word collisions in `clinical_abbreviations.json` include Add (`:7`), Ant (`:11`), ART (`:14`), Flex (`:54`), Glut (`:59`), Hams (`:61`), Mob (`:90`), NAGS (`:96`), PA (`:105`), PROM (`:121`), Quads (`:127`), Rot (`:132`), SNAGS (`:143`), Sup (`:146`), TA (`:148`), TENS (`:149`) and WAD (`:168`); ER (`:46`) is already covered by the filler set.  
PR-MED-001: inconsistent (plan:139, :171, :319 — shared digest stamping and note-context plumbing remain unspecified; PR-MED-009).  
PR-MED-002: inconsistent (plan:115, :176, :330 — actual-reader correction landed, but stale Chrome wording and direct-close omission remain).  
PR-MED-003: inconsistent (plan:176, :330 — cancellation of remaining windows and yield waits landed, but direct window close bypasses cancellation).

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-015** (LOW, validation, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:1772`): Exact duplicate terms evade D1’s one-entry-per-term invariant. Two entries with `term="LBP"`, one containing `"lower back pain"` and another `"lumbar pain"`, pass ownership validation because their owner strings are equal. The lookup then retains the first term definition while accepting both phrase definitions. Even an identical repeated entry is accepted. Evidence: `owner = owners.setdefault(key, entry.term)` followed by `if owner != entry.term:`; lookup construction uses `lookup.setdefault((entry.term.lower(),), entry)`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Reject repeated folded terms before constructing ownership mappings, and test identical and conflicting duplicates with exactly matching case.
- **PR-LOW-016** (LOW, validation, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:1798`): Duplicate JSON object keys are silently discarded before validation. For example, an entry containing `"class":[],"class":"anatomy"` is accepted using the final value instead of producing the broken-install refusal; duplicated top-level fields behave similarly. Evidence: `payload = json.loads(resource.read_bytes())` uses no duplicate-key rejection hook. Recommendation: Fix-now - Reject duplicate object keys during decoding and test raw JSON at both entry and top-level boundaries.
- **PR-LOW-017** (LOW, testing, `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:3143`): The new semantic-invariant tests would still pass if the production import-time `_refuse_register_overlap(CLINICAL_EQUIVALENTS)` call were removed. Invalid registers are checked by directly invoking the helper, while isolated-import tests only exercise the valid shipped register. Evidence: `note_check_module._refuse_register_overlap(CLINICAL_EQUIVALENTS)`, `_refuse(_equivalent("LKP", "left knee pain"))`, and the import test’s `assert result.returncode == 0`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Add an isolated import test with an injected structurally valid but semantically forbidden register, asserting the named broken-install failure.
- **PR-LOW-025** (LOW, record accuracy, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:747`): The record describes entry-wide withholding, but implementation withholds only the matched phrase/term pair. For `("six weeks ago", "Review in 6 weeks")`, the latter still offers `Shorthand: 6 weeks = 6/52`, although both phrases belong to the same duration entry. Evidence: the record says “WITHHOLDS a `duration` entry from the section when any occurrence of its phrase is followed directly by a point-in-time word”; `prose_style.py:439–448` instead uses `pair = f"{' '.join(match.matched)} = {match.entry.term}"`, `withheld.add(pair)` and `pair not in withheld`. Recommendation: Fix-now — describe the existing phrase-specific behavior accurately in the dated record and D.1 residue (3), and pin the mixed-alias case.
- **PR-LOW-026** (LOW, test coverage, `desktop/tests/test_prose_style.py:934`): The budget test does not distinguish the own-voice instruction from the narrative instruction. Both contain exactly the same words in different order, while the mock counts words. Reverting `conditioning_allowance` to always count `NARRATIVE_INSTRUCTION` would still satisfy this assertion. Evidence: `count = MockLanguageModel().count_tokens` followed by the expected `count(own)`; `prose_style.py:320–321` states “the mock counts words.” Recommendation: Fix-now — inject a counting spy that asserts which instruction it receives, or returns distinct counts for the two instructions; also pin the provider’s forwarding of `style=`.
- **PR-LOW-035** (LOW, record accuracy, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:684`): Attempt 2 incorrectly claims `narrative` meets D5 under every reading. Its shared-fixture mean alone is 7.32 s, above the fixed 6.7 s limit; including the nine new sections gives approximately 7.61 s. Evidence: “`narrative` MEETS D5 under every reading” versus table values “7.32 / 9.7” and “8.61 / 9.9”. Recommendation: Fix-now — qualify this historical verdict as meeting the paired reading only, and correct its handoff summary; attempt 5’s closure remains valid.
- **PR-LOW-036** (LOW, practitioner instructions, `docs/testing/prose-register-scoring.md:17`): The procedure omits creating its output directory and incorrectly places redirected summaries on the console. On a fresh run, a missing `C:\prose-register` prevents the command from starting; once created, both summaries enter the report before the JSON because the script prints them to stdout. Evidence: `--verbose > C:\prose-register\report.json`; line 20 says “The first lines on the console are one summary line per style”, and line 27 asks to compare against “the console’s count”. Recommendation: Fix-now — add the directory-creation step and describe the summary lines at the report’s beginning, including that the complete file is not standalone JSON.
- **PR-LOW-037** (LOW, documentation accuracy, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:764`): Residue (3) describes entry-wide withholding, but the implementation withholds an exact matched phrase–term pair. Another phrase belonging to the same duration entry can still be offered within that section. Evidence: “offers no `duration` entry for a section when any occurrence of its phrase is directly followed by a point-in-time word”; the code instead builds `pair = f"{' '.join(match.matched)} = {match.entry.term}"`, uses `withheld.add(pair)`, and filters by pair. Thus “six weeks ago” withholds `six weeks = 6/52`, while a separate “6 weeks” can still offer `6 weeks = 6/52`. Recommendation: Fix-now — narrow D.1, D4’s as-built description and attempt 5’s record to exact-pair withholding; preserve the existing code unless a broader rule is separately intended.

Source: Codex — codex gpt-6-astra medium — build peer review (stage-2.p1, slice B)
Status: Open (2 pending)

Scope:

- job 1: checked the committed extraction, exit callbacks, orphan disposal and close guard; the reviewed paths abort and retain jobs until `finished`. The dated Chrome-discard correction is reflected in the tests.
- job 2: checked input ownership and late-result handling; the holder is popped, pending input is cleared on exit, and slot identity rejects an orphan’s result even after another review starts. The stage’s internal window bound remains outside this slice.
- job 3: checked Save controls, prose scheduling and yield cancellation; suggestions do not directly disable Save, and current prose-job release clears the pending event. No production deadlock identified in these paths.
- job 4: checked signal connections, worker closures, presence gating, status and chooser updates; worker closures contain no widget operations, and orphaned results are rejected before updating widgets.
- job 5: checked the new tests and screen-test diff against their claimed transitions; identified a result-versus-disposal race and timing-based yield-entry assumptions. Static review only; no tests executed.

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-050** (LOW, test reliability, `desktop/tests/test_ui_suggestions.py:589`): The Save test can fail on correct code because result delivery does not guarantee thread disposal. Evidence: `_landed()` waits for `"not screen.suggestion_in_flight and screen._suggest_outcome is not None"` (line 259), followed here by `"assert not screen.is_busy"`. `_JobSlot.release()` appends the reporting job to `_orphaned_jobs`; only the separately delivered `finished` handler removes it. The event pump can return between those signals. Recommendation: Fix-now — retain the landing assertion, then explicitly wait for disposal before asserting that the screen is no longer busy.

- **PR-LOW-051** (LOW, test coverage, `desktop/tests/test_ui_suggestions.py:898`): The yield-wait tests do not establish that the worker entered the wait before closing, so they can exercise abort-before-yield instead of the claimed abort-during-yield path. Evidence: `"stage.release.set()"` followed by `"assert not _process_until(qapp, lambda: stage.windows >= 2, timeout=0.3)"`; the screen-level sibling uses the same pattern at lines 615–616, and the prose-yield test uses a 0.4-second absence check at line 401. A delayed worker satisfies these checks without reaching the yield callback. Fix: wait on the real condition (`_process_until(qapp, lambda: not screen._orphaned_jobs)`) before the assertion; no sleep. Siblings: searched every `is_busy` assertion in the file — the others already wait on `not screen._orphaned_jobs` or `not ... is_busy`, or assert busy on purpose; none other follows a bare `_landed`.

As first applied: The three tests (`TestTheYield::test_a_running_job_waits_while_a_style_job_runs_and_resumes`, `TestTheExitPolicy::test_prepare_close_after_save_inside_a_yield_wait`, `TestThroughTheWindow::test_a_close_after_save_inside_a_yield_wait`) released window 1 and took only an absence of window 2 within 0.3–0.4 s as proof of the wait; a delayed worker could meet the abort at `run_suggestion_stage`'s pre-yield check (`note_suggest.py:414`) and pass without reaching `yield_to`. Fix: the fake stage `_Suggestions` wraps the screen's `yield_to` and counts calls on ENTRY (`yield_calls`; one per window), and each test now first waits for `yield_calls >= 2` — the worker inside the yield before window 2 — before the close, `prepare_close` or the prose release; the absence check stays, now over the in-yield period only (a bound, not the handshake). No production change.
- PR-LOW-074 — VERIFIED; a RECORD fix, not a metric gap. `note_suggest.py:456` is `assessed.update(asked.values())`, run for every window whose completion parsed (not refused), whatever each asked line's answer; `dropped` is the chooser's refusals (`validation.suggestion_pass`: `len(result.suggestions) - len(additions)`), so a missing or malformed answer is in neither count. That is C.1's decided rule ("an answered `none` and an unparsable answer are assessed with no suggestion"), so no column changes meaning (no MUST-PAUSE). Fix: the dated amendment's `Lines not assessed` entry in `docs/testing/validation-harness.md` (CRLF kept) says so, and `validation.SuggestionCounts`' docstring (CRLF kept) carries the same sentence (its sibling wording).

Thus the close tests can still pass without exercising interruption of an existing wait. All three callers retain this timing assumption (lines 416–417, 636–637 and 920–921). - Evidence: `"self.yield_calls += 1  # on entry, before the screen's wait"` precedes `"inner()"`; the screen-close test then uses `"assert not _process_until(qapp, lambda: suggestions.windows >= 2, timeout=0.3)"` before `"screen.prepare_close()"`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Instrument the actual wait primitive with a test-local handshake, acknowledge that the worker reached it while prose is pending and abort is unset, then trigger close or prose release; update the disposition’s wait-entry claim.

**Disposition (stage-2 leg x11, 2026-11-09T12:08:40+11:00; transcribed verbatim above from `.cursor/loops/stage-2-peer-r12-block.md`, the log's last `### Round 12` block; peer round 2 of cap 5).** Round status now: Closed (0 pending) — applied, the composer's pytest owed (verify=composer).
- PR-LOW-086 — VERIFIED and APPLIED, in the TEST only. The claim is right: leg x10's `_Suggestions._counted` read `self.yield_calls += 1  # on entry, before the screen's wait` and only then `inner()`, so `yield_calls >= 2` proved the worker had entered the fake's wrapper, not the screen's `yield_to` — whose wait is `while pending.is_set() and not abort.is_set(): abort.wait(_SUGGESTION_YIELD_POLL_SECONDS)` (`ui/note.py` `_start_suggestion_job`, over the live job's own abort flag `abort = threading.Event()` in `_JobSlot.start`) — and the 0.3–0.4 s absence checks were still the only proof of the wait. Fix: the counter and wrapper are REMOVED; NEW test helper `_watch_yield_wait(screen)` replaces the `wait` method on the live job's abort flag (`screen._suggest_slot.abort`, a plain `threading.Event` instance) with one that sets a test event when called while `_prose_pending` is set and the abort is unset — the wait loop's own condition, so the worker is past it and inside the wait primitive — then calls the real `wait`. It is installed while the worker is held inside window 1 (each test has already waited for `windows == 1`), so it is in place before the worker can reach any wait. The three tests (`TestTheYield::test_a_running_job_waits_while_a_style_job_runs_and_resumes`, `TestTheExitPolicy::test_prepare_close_after_save_inside_a_yield_wait`, `TestThroughTheWindow::test_a_close_after_save_inside_a_yield_wait`) now wait on that event (the timeout a failure bound only), assert `windows == 1` (window 2 can start only once the wait returns), then release the prose job / call `prepare_close` / close the window; the wall-clock absence checks are gone. No production seam: the flag is already reachable through the slot, and `ui/note.py` is unchanged.
- No behaviour change (one test file only) — no C.6 rerun owed.
- CONFIRMED by codex round 13 (peer round 3 of cap 5, 0 findings; Round 13 below).

### Round 16 — 2026-11-09
Source: in-session `/review-loop` (Step H, H1; round 1 of cap 3) — stage-3 leg x3, executor `claude-opus-5-5`, four read-only lens reviewers run in parallel (A: the two job slots, the exit policy and the shared lock; B: the chooser and learning; C: the register in Check 5; D: the harness and its documents) plus Step H's carried item; every finding re-verified by the executor against the code before its disposition.
Status: Closed (0 pending) — 12 fixed (code, tests, documents); 3 not fixed with reasons; 3 to the practitioner (the MUST-PAUSE on Current State).

Scope: `git diff 3a42301 -- desktop/ config_defaults/ extension/ scripts/` (38 files) and the uncommitted Phase D documents. Lens A: `ui/note.py` (`_JobSlot`, `_begin_suggestions`, `_start_suggestion_job`, `_release_style_job`, `_dispose`, `prepare_close`, `is_busy`, `clear()`), `ui/main_window.py` (`closeEvent`, `_on_transcript_closed`), `language_model.py`, `note_suggest.run_suggestion_stage`, `ui/models.build_suggestion_stage`, `test_ui_suggestions.py`, `test_ui_prose_stage.py`. Lens B: `ui/models.eligible_utterances` and the marks / counts / status strings, the chooser, Add, Undo and the learning gate in `ui/note.py` and `ui/note_review.py`, `note_suggest.SuggestionInput.from_request` / `parse_suggestions`. Lens C: `note_config` (the lexicon loader), `note_check` (`canonicalise`, `fidelity_verdict`, the semantic refusals, the frame), `note.py` (`section_other_voice`, the digest), `prose_style.py` (the Shorthand / Speaker lines), `logging_setup.py`, the lexicon file (read only). Lens D: `validation.py` (`suggestion_pass`, `SuggestionCounts`, the columns, the CLI), `scripts/measure-prose-fixtures.py`, the harness tests, and the harness claims in `docs/testing/validation-harness.md`, `scripts/README.md` and AGENTS.md step 12. Process note: lens B reported that its `git diff` calls were refused and that it ran one read-only `sed … | grep` over this plan outside its permitted commands; it wrote nothing. No reviewer opened anything under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, `validation/heldout*` or `C:\ncd-*`.

Findings (each re-verified; the quoted line is the one the executor read):
- **MED-001** (lens A-1, the yield, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:2370`): `_release_style_job` cleared `_prose_pending` (`self._prose_pending.clear()`) at the TOP of `_on_style_done`, while the re-render it may start is set only at `:2436` (`self._start_style_stage()` → `:2350` `self._prose_pending.set()`), after the bind, the body re-render and `_refresh_warnings`. A suggestion worker polling every 0.05 s in `yield_to` can leave its wait in that gap and start a whole window, so the re-render (and Save's re-enable) waits that window — Flow 2's "runs as soon as the suggestion job yields" lost on exactly that path (D8's outer bound still held). The docstring at `:2366` claimed the opposite ("sets `_prose_pending` again first").
- **MED-002** (lens A-2, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_suggest.py:420-444`): the last abort check (`:422`) precedes `model.count_tokens` (`:432`) and `_fit_window` (`:433`), each of which takes the model's lock (`language_model.py:363` `with self._lock:`); nothing re-checks before `raw = model.complete(` (`:440`). An exit during a lock wait (the previous review's orphaned prose section holds it) lets a full NEW window of transcript go to the model after the key is gone — more than C13's "in-flight window".
- **LOW-001** (lens A-3, `ui/main_window.py:1491` with `ui/note.py:3203` `reviewing = self._draft is not None and not self._note_saved`): `prepare_close()` aborted a live job even when the close is refused regardless — an X-click during an UNSAVED review stops that review's suggestions for good (`SUGGESTION_STOPPED_LINE`), though D8's stated reason is the after-Save case only.
- **LOW-002** (lens A-4, `desktop/tests/test_ui_suggestions.py:654-656`): `test_prepare_close_after_save_inside_a_yield_wait` passed with a no-op `prepare_close` (the live job is not counted by `is_busy`, and the job stays in its wait with the flag set, so `windows == 1` held too).
- **LOW-003** (lens A-5, `ui/note.py:1142` `self._prose_pending.clear()` in `clear()`): no test covers it — the only thing that frees the NEXT review's suggestion job while the previous review's orphaned prose job is still running (an orphan's release returns False and clears nothing).
- **LOW-004** (lens A-6): (a) `_dispose` (`ui/note.py:2387-2394`) never touched `_prose_pending`, so a style job ending without reaching its handler's last step (a raising handler; a `BaseException`, which `TaskThread.run` does not catch — `tasks.py:39` `except Exception`) could leave a suggestion worker waiting for good; (b) `build_suggestion_stage`'s `cache.get(model_factory)` (`ui/models.py:4318`) runs before any abort check, so a close right after Save during the process's first load waits the load, not one window.
- **LOW-005** (lens B-1, `ui/note.py:2264-2272`: `changed = data != self._chooser_segment` … `suggestion = self._accepted_suggestions.get(segment_index) if changed else None`): when a job lands, the line ALREADY showing in the chooser gets its mark but not its pre-selected section (re-picking the same entry fires no change), so the mark and the "to:" box can disagree until another line is chosen — impossible when the filtered list holds one entry. An Add goes to the section shown; nothing wrong enters the note.
- **LOW-006** (lens B-2, `note_suggest.py:456` `assessed.update(asked.values())`): a completion that parses but answers few asked numbers counts the window's every asked line as assessed.
- **LOW-007** (lens B-3, unverified, `ui/models.py:3015-3016` with `SUGGESTED_LINE_MARK` `:2726`): a long section title after `[flagged] ` may be cut by the open list's middle elision on a narrow popup.
- **LOW-008** (lens C-1, `note_config.py` `canonical` → `self.phrases[0]`; `note_check.py:1938` `folded.extend(entry.canonical)`): a one-token abbreviation folds to separate qualifier tokens, so a swap between sibling entries passes Check 5 on ONE line — "UMN signs, lower limb" → "LMN signs, upper limb", "FWB, partial ROM" → "PWB, full ROM", "NWB R leg, smoker" → "Weight bearing R leg, non smoker" (`non` is not in `_NEGATIONS`, `note.py:2446`). Under `narrative-v2` all three failed (`umn`, `fwb`, `nwb` were whole tokens rule (a) required). The token-not-attachment residue reaching a folded term on a single line, wider than (r1)'s two-line example.
- **LOW-009** (lens C-2, `note_check.py:1993-1995` `addable = addable | SPEAKER_FRAME_TOKENS`): the frame tokens are admitted anywhere and any number of times in a framed section, not only as the opening "Pt reports" — "back hurts when lifting at work" → "Pt reports back hurts when lifting patient at work"; a clinician's "Refer for therapy" in a mixed section → "Refer for PT therapy" (an added physiotherapy reading, (r5)'s reverse).
- **LOW-010** (lens C-3, `note_config.py:1758-1766`): for a duration entry the loader checked only the FIRST phrase's count (`if phrases[0] != (str(weeks), unit)`) and every phrase's unit; a later `"13 weeks"` under `3/52` would fold to `3, weeks` (prose "3/52" for input "13 weeks"). The shipped file is pinned by `test_every_duration_entry_expands_exactly`; the loader is the runtime refusal.
- **LOW-011** (lens C-4, `note_config.py:1577-1578`): the comment "Widening the list is the practitioner's dated decision and a ``PROMPT_VERSION`` bump (C2); narrowing it is routine." implied a narrowing needs no `PROMPT_VERSION` bump, against D1 / C2 ("a change to the shipped list bumps `PROMPT_VERSION`"); and no test ties the file's content to the version (a same-count edit passes the count pins).
- **LOW-012** (lens C-5, unverified, `note.py:1104-1108`): the speaker flag is computed from `assertion.speaker`, which Check 3 treats as provider-supplied display attribution; a provider labelling a clinician line as another speaker would turn the frame on.
- **LOW-013** (lens C-6, `clinical_equivalents.json:67` `"Inv"` → "inversion", `:7` `"AP"` → "anteroposterior"): two second meanings outside (r6)'s seven — `Inv` (investigations), `AP` (assessment and plan; action potential); typed "Inv: bloods, X-ray" → prose "Inversion: bloods, X-ray" passes.
- **LOW-014** (lens D-1, `docs/testing/validation-harness.md:230`, "## Rule v6"): the recorded `confirm-all` cost named only the R3 / next-patient mechanism; C.6 (this plan, `:1143`) also records two misheard lines left out under `off` entering through a suggestion (`h2-15` newly `material_wrong`; `h3-13`'s fact 2 taking `heldout-3`'s R1 to 2 over 1).
- **LOW-015** (lens D-2, `docs/testing/validation-harness.md:45` and `scripts/README.md:111-113`): "asks the local model for a section for every left-out line" — the input is cut at the next-patient boundary (`note_config.py:3183`), so lines at or past a detected boundary are never asked.
- **LOW-016** (Step H's carried item, from codex round 8 PR-LOW-016's disposition): the two older shipped-file loaders decoded with plain `json.loads` — `note_config.py:1516` `payload = json.loads(resource.read_bytes())` (the two vocabularies) and `note.py:1590` (the same, the section cues) — so a repeated key in a shipped file silently kept the last (a section repeated would import as complete with its first list lost).

Checked, no defect (the reviewers' lists, re-read where a disposition rests on them): `_JobSlot.start/release/orphan` (`ui/note.py:568-619`: a job is never orphaned twice; a late signal after `orphan()` or `release()` is not current); an orphan's result returns before any widget (`_on_suggest_done` / `_on_suggest_failed`); `begin_review` and `show_saved_note` call `clear()` first; `clear()` orphans both slots and resets every suggestion field; every style-handler path ends in `_start_due_suggestion`; the lock is not re-entrant and never nested, `cache.get` holds a separate lock (no deadlock); acceptance is `key in allowed and not past_boundary` through the same `admissible_sections` call as `add_line`; a line in the note gets no mark; the input boundary and the chooser's boundary come from the one `next_patient_boundary`; stale pairs cannot outlive `clear()`, and trimming is refused while a review is open; Undo returns the mark; the learning gate refuses another speaker's line, a shadow review and anything before Save; no model text crosses (`SuggestionStageResult` holds ids, keys and counts; a failure shows its type name only); `parse_suggestions` holds D7's form; Check 5 folds both sides alike and counts polarity and protected tokens after the fold; the frame is off unless the section is flagged; the digest binds the speaker flag; `shorthand_line` offers phrase matches only and withholds a duration before a point-in-time word; no logger in `note_check` / `note_config` / `prose_style`; `suggestion_pass` builds its job and accepts through `eligible_utterances` exactly as the Note tab; under `off` every report row is byte-identical; `SuggestionCounts` validation; the CLI's refusals; `measure-prose-fixtures.py`'s recordings line up with outcomes.

**Dispositions (stage-3 leg x3, 2026-11-09T13:26:51+11:00).** Line numbers below are the working tree's after the fixes.
- MED-001 — VERIFIED and FIXED. `_release_style_job` no longer clears the flag (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:2357-2376`, the docstring corrected); `_start_due_suggestion` — the last step of every style-handler path — clears it only when no style job holds the slot (`:2548-2558`), so a re-render started in the same handler keeps it set throughout. Test: `desktop/tests/test_ui_suggestions.py` `TestTheYield::test_a_re_render_keeps_the_yield_flag_set_across_the_hand_off` (records every `set` / `clear` on the screen's flag across a stale-digest report and its re-render; `["set", "clear"]` with the fix, `["clear", "set", …]` without).
- MED-002 — VERIFIED and FIXED. `note_suggest.py:439-444`: after the window's token counts and before `windows_run += 1` / `model.complete`, `if abort is not None and abort(): reason = "aborted"; break` (the `finally` still adds the attempt's seconds; the lines stay not assessed). Test: `desktop/tests/test_note_suggest.py` `test_an_abort_during_the_token_counts_sends_no_window` (`:567`; a model whose `count_tokens` flips the abort: no `complete` call, `windows_run == 0`, `lines_not_assessed == 2`). The prose stage's sibling (`prose_style.py:721` checks before its section's counts, not before `:756`'s `complete`) is pre-existing — note-learning Phase H round 24's shape, outside this plan's change — NOT fixed here: its orphan's result is dropped and threat-model surface 17 already names "the section call in progress"; recorded for H2 to weigh.
- LOW-001 — VERIFIED and FIXED inside D8: `NoteScreen.prepare_close` returns at once while the review is open and unsaved (`ui/note.py:2643-2658`) — that review refuses the close on its own through `is_busy`'s `reviewing`, so aborting gained nothing; the after-Save case D8 names, and every caller's order (`prepare_close` BEFORE `is_busy`, `main_window.py:1491`), are unchanged. Test: `TestTheExitPolicy::test_prepare_close_during_an_unsaved_review_leaves_the_job_running`. The other half (another screen busy AFTER Save) is NOT changed: after Save the suggestions no longer serve a review, and D8's step is exactly for that case. - LOW-002 — VERIFIED and FIXED: the test now asserts, right after `prepare_close()`, `not screen.suggestion_in_flight` and `SUGGESTION_STOPPED_LINE`, then waits for `not screen._orphaned_jobs` (`test_ui_suggestions.py:731-737`).
- LOW-003 — VERIFIED and FIXED (coverage): `TestTheYield::test_the_next_review_never_waits_on_an_orphaned_prose_job` (`test_ui_suggestions.py:437`): a held narrative prose job, Cancel review, a new review with no renderable section — its job lands while the orphan is still held (without `clear()`'s line it would wait in its yield until the next exit).
- LOW-004 — (a) VERIFIED and FIXED: `_dispose` clears the flag whenever no style job holds the slot (`ui/note.py:2399-2405`, the comment from `:2400`) — `finished` is queued after the job's own signal, so it runs after any handler; the flag is set only immediately before a style job starts, in the same GUI call, so the backstop never drops a pending flag. Covered by the tests above, which pass through `_dispose` on every job. (b) NOT FIXED: the first load is bounded and already named on this plan (D9's derivation, "about 10 s more if the close lands during the model's first load"); the prose orphan has the same shape.

Trust boundaries examined: the transcript and confirmed note → the prompt (and `special=True` tokenisation); the completion → the parsers → the note / screen / log / audit row / exception / report; the session key's destruction vs the worker threads; the shipped and user config files → the loaders; the frozen bundle and the channel's version strings. Nothing under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, `validation/heldout*` or `C:\ncd-*` was opened; the lexicon's content and the prompt text were not changed.

Findings:
- **SEC-001** (LOW; lens A; Triage: Fix-now; Fix route: `/fix`, applied) — the `<think>` strip-and-refuse pair let a nested or pre-opened block's reasoning through. `language_model.THINK_BLOCK_RE` (lazy) on `<think><think>a</think>b</think>` removes `<think><think>a</think>` and leaves `b</think>`; a completion holding only a closing tag (`reasoning…</think>answer`, a thinking template's shape) has nothing stripped; the refusal (`note_suggest.py:299-301`, `prose_style.py:561-563` before the fix) tested only for a left `<think>`, so `b` was parsed — in the suggestions, as answers that win over the real ones (first answer per number), in the prose, as prose then judged by Check 5. Bounded today: the pinned model is the non-thinking release and a transcript cannot inject `<think>` into the suggestion prompt (`_prompt_safe`). Fixed: one shared `language_model.strip_think_blocks(raw) -> str | None` (strips the closed blocks, None when `<think>` or `</think>` is left, case-insensitive; `CLOSE_THINK_TAG` beside `OPEN_THINK_TAG`), called by both parsers, which refuse on None (the suggestion parser returns None, the prose parser `""` — the existing refusal paths). Pattern siblings: searched `THINK_BLOCK_RE`, `OPEN_THINK_TAG`, `<think>`, `</think>` across `desktop/src/` — these two parsers only. Tests: `test_note_suggest.py` `TestTheParser::test_a_refused_completion_yields_nothing` (+3: nested, lone close, upper-case lone close), `TestTheMovedNames::test_the_two_names_are_language_models_and_shared` (now pins the one shared helper and its three refusals); `test_prose_style.py` `TestParseSectionProse::test_a_left_close_tag_is_refused` (+2). Regression risk: a completion holding a stray `</think>` is now refused — the non-thinking release does not emit one; nothing re-measured (no prompt, lexicon or Check 5 change; a run-9 completion holding the tag would be refused under the rule in force for it). /fix decision: Applied.
- **SEC-002** (LOW; lenses A and D; Triage: MUST-PAUSE Q2 for the code, the records Fix-now and applied) — the prose prompt is not made prompt-safe, and two records said otherwise. `prose_style.py:474` writes each confirmed line as `- {text}` and `_render_conditioning` (`:354-360`) writes own voice's shorthand tokens and exemplars into the system text, none through a `_prompt_safe` step; the runtime tokenises with `special=True` (`language_model.py:343`, `:369`), so a line spelling `<|im_end|>` becomes a live control token. The plan's Deferred item "`_prompt_safe` on the prose prompt builder" (decided Defer by the practitioner on 2026-11-08) rated the risk on a claim that is false — "already refused as an echoed marker on the way out": `parse_section_prose` refuses only the two list markers. And the threat model said the prose stage "never see[s] transcript text" (`threat-model.md`, surface 17 THE INPUT) and, out of scope, that the transcript reaches the model "only through the section suggestions" — but a transcript-provenance confirmed line is a verbatim quote. What bounds it (verified): Check 5 reads the `ProseInput`, never the prompt (`prose_style.py:776-781`), so a forged turn, Speaker or Shorthand line cannot widen the gate; recogniser words cannot spell `<|` (special tokens excluded in `mark_words`); a typed line, a learned shorthand token (`_ShorthandToken` allows `<|`) or a pasted past note (`_ExemplarText` refuses control characters only) is the practitioner's own. Applied now (records only): the threat model's surface 17 gains "THE PROSE PROMPT IS NOT MADE PROMPT-SAFE" and its out-of-scope narrowing says "the unconfirmed transcript"; the Deferred item carries a dated correction beside its unedited wrong line. The CODE fix — a shared `prompt_safe` on the prose prompt's rows and conditioning, never on Check 5's inputs — changes the prompt (a new `PROMPT_VERSION`, R.5's paired timing and R.6's 50 repeated), which this pass may not do: put to the practitioner as the MUST-PAUSE's Q2, since the deferral was decided on the wrong reason. /fix decision: records Applied; code Pending (MUST-PAUSE Q2). **Disposition (2026-11-09T16:53+11:00): Q2 decided (a) by the practitioner, "(a) Keep it deferred (Recommended)" — the code stays deferred (the Deferred item's re-decision line); the records stand as applied. Closed.**
- **SEC-003** (LOW; lens A; Fix-now, applied) — the suggestion prompt's sanitising held only because both callers build their lines through `SuggestionInput.from_request` (`note_suggest.py:241`, the only `_prompt_safe` call): `build_window_prompt` wrote `line.suggestion_text` raw, and `SuggestionLine` is a public frozen dataclass. Fixed: `build_window_prompt` passes each line's words through `_prompt_safe` again at the prompt's one builder — idempotent (verified by hand over every `TestPromptSafe.SPECIALS` shape: a split `<` is followed by a space and a split `>` preceded by one), so a `from_request` line's prompt is byte-identical and C.6's `off` reproduction is untouched. Tests: `TestPromptSafe::test_the_safe_text_is_a_fixed_point` (+11, one per special) and `::test_the_prompt_builder_makes_a_directly_built_line_safe` (+1). /fix decision: Applied.
- **SEC-004** (LOW; lens B; Triage: MUST-PAUSE Q1 — D8 is LOCKED) — the suggestion job keeps running after Save although nothing can use its result. `NoteScreen.save` (`ui/note.py:2749`) sets `_note_saved` and leaves `_suggest_slot` alone; Save closes the chooser, the section box and Add (`_edits_open`), and this plan's own record says "after Save the suggestions no longer serve a review". The job runs window after window to its cap, holding the pre-boundary transcript; at Complete or Discard the key goes inside `_complete_locked` BEFORE `_on_transcript_closed` calls `clear()` (`main_window.py:2084`) and sets the abort, so (s1)'s post-key lifetime exists after Save only because the job was not stopped at Save — and a window boundary falling inside that synchronous gap starts a fresh window after the key is gone. Verified: no guard covers it (`_refuse_while_generating` guards the lease, which Save releases; the suggestion worker is not a lease holder; `prepare_close` runs on window close only). D8 lists the exits and is silent on Save. **Disposition (2026-11-09T16:53+11:00): Q1 decided (b) by the practitioner, "(b) Stop at Save and Delete (Recommended)" — D8 amended (its dated line); BUILT by stage-3 leg x6: `NoteScreen.save` calls `_stop_suggestions(models.SUGGESTION_SAVED_LINE)` right after `self._note_saved = True` (`ui/note.py`, the same call that released the lease, so no Complete or Discard can run in between); `prepare_close`'s body moved into the shared `_stop_suggestions` (the slot's existing `orphan`, never a new path). Complete and Discard's order re-checked: both are refused under the lease (`session.py:1342`, `:1478`), released only by Save, Delete or Cancel, so after this change neither can destroy the key before the job was told to stop — no `MainWindow` change. Tests: `test_ui_suggestions.py` `TestTheExitPolicy::test_save_is_allowed_and_then_stops_the_job` (the job live AT `on_save`, aborted after it — fails reverted), `::test_a_failed_save_keeps_the_job_running`, `::test_save_inside_a_yield_wait_breaks_the_wait`, `::test_prepare_close_after_save_finds_the_job_already_stopped`; `TestThroughTheWindow::test_complete_and_discard_after_save_find_the_job_told_to_stop` (×2: the abort set at the moment the Transcript tab's key-destroying callback runs — fails reverted), the two close-after-Save tests and the Chrome next-Start test updated (the job stopped by Save). - **SEC-005** (LOW; lens B; MUST-PAUSE Q1, with SEC-004) — Delete note and complete destroys the key while the job is live: `NoteScreen.abandon` (`ui/note.py:2880-2892`) calls `_on_abandon()` → `SessionController.complete_without_note` (`session.py:1379-1421`, `_complete_locked` under the held lease — the key deleted) and only then `clear()`. It is the one key-destroying exit that can come BEFORE Save, and (s1) names only Complete and Discard (lens D dropped this as "the other exits retire the session without destroying its key" — verified wrong for this exit). **Disposition (2026-11-09T16:53+11:00): Q1 (b), as SEC-004 — BUILT by stage-3 leg x6: `NoteScreen.abandon` calls `_stop_suggestions(models.SUGGESTION_DELETE_LINE)` BEFORE `self._on_abandon()` (a refused completion keeps the review without the job). Tests: `TestTheExitPolicy::test_delete_stops_the_job_before_its_callback` (the abort set and the slot empty AT the callback — fails reverted), `::test_a_failed_delete_keeps_the_review_without_the_job`; the existing `test_an_exit_mid_run_cancels_the_remaining_windows[abandon]` (screen and window) unchanged. Documents: the threat model's exit-policy bullet and (s1), flow 29, the retention row, `docs/design-system.md`'s status lines. Closed.**
- **SEC-006** (LOW; lens B INFO-1, raised to LOW as a record over-claim; Fix-now, applied) — the stated post-exit lifetime ("until its ONE in-flight window returns": (s1), flow 29, the retention row) leaves out the model-load phase: under `clean` the first review of a run loads the 2.3 GiB model on the suggestion thread (`ui/models.py:4318`, `cache.get` under the cache lock, no abort check), after `holder.pop()` (`ui/note.py:2577`) has handed the input over; an exit during the load orphans a job with NO window in flight whose frame keeps the whole input until the load ends (tens of seconds), when `note_suggest.py:414`'s abort stops it before any call. Applied (records only): the threat model's (s1), flow 29 of `data-flow-map.md` and the retention schedule's "In-memory suggestion state" row name the load phase. The code alternative (pop the input only after the load) was not taken — the lifetime is the same size as the named one, in a different phase.
- **SEC-007** (LOW; lens B INFO-2 (a); Fix-now, applied) — `ui.models.UtteranceChoice` (extended by Phase C with `suggested`) and `EditableLine` carry a transcript line's first words in `label`, and no field of either was a registered tripwire signature — backstop only (no log call reaches them: the five diffed modules hold no logger). Fixed: `allowed_sections`, the one distinctive field both hold, registered in its three forms in `logging_setup._PAYLOAD_SIGNATURES` (no `ALLOWED_KEYS` rendering holds it). Test: `test_note_suggest.py` `TestTheTripwire::test_every_rendering_of_a_chooser_entry_or_edit_row_is_dropped` (+1). Not applied: INFO-2 (b), registering the two prompt headers as signatures — a bare prompt string has no rendering path (no logger in the three model modules) and a header would be the first non-field-name signature; recorded, not built.
- **SEC-008** (LOW; lens C; Triage: Open follow-up — outside the Agreed Scope) — a repeated JSON key in a clinician-edited config file is not refused: `note_config._parse_config_blob` (`:963-967`) validates with `model_validate_json` and no pre-decode hook, while round 16's `_decode_shipped_json` covers only the four import-time shipped loaders. Older than this plan and unchanged by it; refusing it changes what the installed app accepts from the practitioner's own files. Recorded under "Deferred — Actionable Later" ("A repeated JSON key in a clinician-edited config file"; `Risk if deferred: minor`).
- **SEC-009** (LOW; lens D LOW-2; Fix-now, applied) — the threat model's "One module, one call site (C7)" said the transcript reaches the model "only through `run_suggestion_stage`'s one `complete` call"; `_fit_window` (`note_suggest.py:363`) also passes each attempt's window text to `count_tokens` (the runtime's tokenizer, `special=True`; no inference state, no output). The offline claim holds (the real-model no-sockets leg runs a whole window, counts included). Applied: the bullet names the token counts and says "the unconfirmed transcript"; flow 29 already says "one token count per attempt" (unchanged).
- **SEC-010** (LOW; lens D LOW-3; Fix-now, applied) — the threat model says no register entry holds "a first-person token", but `note_check._register_refusal` (`:1897`) checked the PHRASES' tokens only; a term was caught only through `ordinary_words`, which lacks `mine`, `i've`, `i'm` and `we've` (all in `FIRST_PERSON_TOKENS`). No live exposure (the shipped lexicon is pinned to `PROMPT_VERSION` by its blob id; no shipped term is first person). Fixed: the check reads `(term, *phrase_tokens)`, message "it holds a first-person token" (the existing test matches "first-person"). Test: `test_note_check.py` `TestRegisterSemanticInvariants::test_a_first_person_term_is_refused` (+1, `Mine`). No lexicon content changes; the import-time refusal still passes the shipped file (no term folds to a first-person token).
- **SEC-011** (LOW; lens D LOW-4; Fix-now, applied) — `docs/security/intended-use.md:25-27` said the prose styles' shorthand is "never a new finding", but the register's named residues (r4), (r5), (r7), (r8) pass a changed finding (a sibling abbreviation swap, a second meaning). Applied: "designed never to add a finding: Check 5 refuses most changes, and for the changes it is known to pass … the clinician's reading of every prose section before Save is the control (surface 17, … residues (r1)–(r8))".
- Checked and clean (by category): **AuthN/AuthZ** — single-user local app; acceptance only through `eligible_utterances`' ownership rule (`ui/models.py:3013`), the harness through the same rule (D6). **Injection & taint** — the suggestion prompt's only untrusted string is `suggestion_text` through `_prompt_safe` (C0/C1/DEL spaced, angle-bracket runs split, `str.split()` folds U+2028/2029); speaker is a role label; section keys are typed; the parser takes ASCII `[0-9]{1,4}` and canonical keys only, numbers outside the window's asked set refused, first answer wins, the segment index from the input never from the model. **Secrets & sensitive data** — no logger in `note_suggest.py` / `prose_style.py` / `language_model.py`; `LanguageModelError` and `_on_suggest_failed` carry the type only; `SuggestionStageResult` and `SuggestionCounts` hold ids, keys and counts; refused prose is written as empty; the harness prints fixed text or type names. **Outbound / offline** — no socket, http, urllib, `huggingface_hub`, `from_pretrained`, `CDLL` or `LLAMA_CPP_LIB_PATH` use in the diff; the model path under the channel's models root with UNC/size/SHA-256 refusals before the runtime import; the no-sockets legs not vacuous (mock legs polled inside the call; real legs ≥ 3 polls). **Custody & cross-session** — `_JobSlot.release` identity check, `clear()` orphans before any new `begin_review`, `_on_suggest_done` drops a non-current result; the 24 h sweep's `custody_protected_ids`; recovery offers no generation; a Chrome discard never reaches a review. **Resource** — `max_tokens` ≤ 336 (suggestions) / 768 (prose); the window budgeted by the runtime's tokenizer, halved on overflow; the 240 s cap before each window; every loop step advances. **Loaders** — duplicate keys, bad UTF-8 and deep nesting refused at import; `schema_version` by `type(...) is int`; the lexicon read from the package only (never the user config folder); the frozen bundle globs `config_defaults/*.json` and the build audit pins the lexicon. **Dependencies & config** — no new dependency; the six version strings agree at 0.3.6; no debug flag enabled (`verbose=False`).
- Gates: ruff "All checks passed!", mypy "Success: no issues found in 67 source files". Line endings kept (`git ls-files --eol`: none `w/mixed`). No prompt text, lexicon content, Check 5 fold rule or harness count changed; SEC-003's prompt is byte-identical for every `from_request` line; SEC-001 changes only a completion holding a stray `</think>`. No R.5 / R.6 / C.6 rerun is owed by the applied fixes (Q2 (b), if chosen, owes R.5 and R.6).

- **PR-LOW-300** (LOW, retention documentation, `docs/security/retention-schedule.md:52`): The prose-state row still limits prompt/completion references to one model call, although the caller retains those strings afterward. Evidence: “one section's PROMPT and its COMPLETION exist for the duration of that one call”. `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/prose_style.py:755` assigns `system_text, user_text`; `:766` assigns `raw`; these locals remain referenced during subsequent section processing and final verdict construction until overwritten or `render` returns at `:804`. Runtime clearing at `language_model.py:367` does not clear the caller’s Python locals. The sibling wording at `docs/security/data-flow-map.md:825` says “prompt and completion for that `complete` call”. Recommendation: Fix-now - Distinguish runtime inference-state clearing from caller-held strings surviving until replacement or render return, matching the distinction already corrected for suggestions.
  - **Confirmed by codex round 21 (2026-11-09, recorded by stage-3 leg x8): its job 1 — runtime clearing distinguished from the caller-held strings in all three documents.**
  - **Disposition (stage-3 leg x7, 2026-11-09T17:31:11+11:00): VERIFIED and FIXED.** The code: `prose_style.py:755` `system_text, user_text = build_section_prompt(`, `:766` `raw = self._model.complete(`, `:776` `prose[section_key] = parse_section_prose(raw, section_key)`, `:804` `return ProseResult(self._style, tuple(outcomes), warnings)` — the three locals stay referenced past each call until the next section's assignment or the return. Fixed: `docs/security/retention-schedule.md` row 52 ("exist in the runtime for the duration of that one call … — and, as strings, in the stage's own frame past that call (`ProseStyleProvider.render`'s `system_text` / `user_text` and `raw`), until the next section's call replaces them or `render` returns"); `docs/security/data-flow-map.md` flow 17 (`:828-837`, the same distinction, pointing at flow 29's); and the same over-claim found in `docs/security/threat-model.md` surface 17's offline item (3) ("one section's prompt and completion pass through it … so what remains is the weights") — qualified "in the RUNTIME; the stage's own frame keeps that section's prompt and completion strings past the call …". No other place states the prose lifetime (searched "for the duration of that", "prompt and completion", "PROMPT and its COMPLETION" across `docs/`).
