#### Slice E (verbatim)
Source: Codex — codex gpt-6-astra medium — build peer review (stage-3.p2, slice E)

- `docs/design-system.md`: checked chooser marks, filtering, counts and Save/Delete status strings; implementation agrees except for the remaining close-wait claim below.
- `scripts/README.md`: checked CLI flags, missing-model refusals, pre-boundary inputs and finalisation policy; supported by current harness code.
- `CHANGELOG.md`: checked loader refusals, residue count, release qualification and input/lifetime descriptions; two stale descriptions are included in the findings below.
- `AGENTS.md`: checked harness options, subsystem pointers, residue count and built-versus-owed records; no additional defect established.
- `PLAN.md`: checked Phase 3B’s delivery description and pending run 9; transcript-input novelty claim remains inaccurate.

- Job 3: checked Rounds 14–19 and their dispositions; findings concern missed propagation of SEC-002/SEC-006 documentation corrections, not reopening accepted or deferred behavior. Static review only; no writes, scripts, tests or network commands.

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-330** (LOW, documentation accuracy, `PLAN.md:112`): The delivery records still imply transcript text reaches the language model only through the new suggestions feature. Evidence: “the transcript reaches the local model for the first time”; sibling `CHANGELOG.md:8` says “the transcript as input to the local language model for section suggestions only”. Current `prose_style.py:286` takes `tuple(a.text for a in section.note_assertions)` and `:474` places those words into the prose prompt; the same path exists at `edb2724`. Transcript-provenance confirmed lines therefore already reached the model through prose. Round 19 SEC-002 explicitly corrected this distinction elsewhere. Recommendation: Fix-now - Qualify both records as **unconfirmed transcript** input and preserve the existing confirmed-note prose path.
  - **Confirmed by codex round 21 (2026-11-09, recorded by stage-3 leg x8): its job 2 — all six sites qualified as unconfirmed-transcript input; no unqualified first-use claim left.**
  - **Disposition (stage-3 leg x7, 2026-11-09T17:31:11+11:00): VERIFIED and FIXED.** The code: `prose_style.py:474` writes each confirmed line as `- {text}` (the round-19 SEC-002 finding) and the confirmed texts come from the note's assertions — a transcript-provenance assertion's text is the verbatim transcript span. Fixed: `PLAN.md:112` ("the UNCONFIRMED transcript reaches the local model for the first time (a confirmed note line, often a verbatim transcript quote, already reached it through the prose styles)"), `CHANGELOG.md:8` ("the UNCONFIRMED transcript as input to the local language model, for section suggestions only (confirmed note lines, often verbatim transcript quotes, already reached it through the prose styles)"); the same claim found and qualified in `docs/security/threat-model.md` (its introduction, `:41-43`, and the "Phase 3B (coverage)" opening, `:2167-2170`; the section heading is left as it is, the body qualifying it) and `docs/security/data-flow-map.md` (flow 17's narrowing note, `:789-797`, and the non-flow "No transcript text to the language model FROM THE PROSE STAGE", `:1745-1750`). Searched "transcript (as input|is input|reaches the local|becomes input)", "for the first time", "section suggestions only", "only through", "never see[s] transcript" across `PLAN.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `docs/`: no other instance (AGENTS.md and the review guide make no such claim).
- **PR-LOW-331** (LOW, lifecycle documentation, `docs/design-system.md:213`): The design-system page still bounds a suggestion-related close wait by an inference window, omitting the accepted initial-model-load case. Evidence: “a running one never holds Complete, Discard or a close for more than the one window in flight”; sibling `CHANGELOG.md:8` describes the “window-long lifetime after Complete or Discard”. `ui/models.py:4326` loads the model before `run_suggestion_stage` can check abort, while `ui/note.py`’s `is_busy` includes `bool(self._orphaned_jobs)`. Save during that load can therefore leave an orphan that holds close and retains input until loading finishes, with no window in flight—the case recorded in Round 19 SEC-006. Recommendation: Fix-now - Distinguish nonblocking Complete/Discard from close waiting for orphan completion, and include initial model loading alongside an in-flight window in both descriptions.
  - **Confirmed by codex round 21 (2026-11-09, recorded by stage-3 leg x8): its job 3 — Complete and Discard never held; a close and another recording's review wait for the orphan, the initial load included; D8's stop at Save and before Delete preserved.**
  - **Disposition (stage-3 leg x7, 2026-11-09T17:31:11+11:00): VERIFIED and FIXED.** The code: `ui/models.py:4326` `model = cache.get(model_factory) if cache is not None else model_factory()` runs before `run_suggestion_stage(suggestion_input, model, abort=abort, yield_to=yield_to)` (`:4330`) can read the abort, and `ui/note.py` `is_busy` returns `… or bool(self._orphaned_jobs)`; Complete and Discard never read `is_busy` (the Transcript tab's buttons, `main_window.py:1267-1268`). Fixed: `docs/design-system.md:212-218` ("a running one never holds Complete or Discard at all. Once it is stopped … it still finishes the one step it is in, and until then a window close or opening another recording's review waits: one window in flight — or, when it was stopped while the language model was still loading for the first time, the rest of that load"); `CHANGELOG.md:8` ("the post-exit lifetime of the input (until the one window in flight, or the model's initial load, ends) after Complete, Discard or Delete note and complete"); the same omission found and fixed in `docs/security/threat-model.md`'s exit-policy bullet (the orphan busy until its window returns "— or, orphaned while the model is still loading on its thread …, until that load ends …; Complete and Discard never read it and are never held"), AGENTS.md's section-suggestions pointer, and this plan's D8 amendment line ("— or, orphaned during the model's first load, the rest of that load"). Residue (s1), flow 29 and retention row 53 already name the load (round 19 SEC-006). Searched "in-flight window", "window in flight", "wait at most", "at most one window" across `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `PLAN.md` and `docs/`.

Today `i`, `i've`, `i'm`, `me`, `my`, `we` are NOT connectives, so a patient line's prose must keep them: "I've had this pain in my lower back" can become "Pt reports pain in lower back" only if the first person is glue under the frame. Options: (a) **the swap (Recommended)** — under `speaker_frame` the first-person tokens (i, i've, i'm, me, my, mine, we, we've, our; the exact set pinned) are droppable in rule (a), the frame tokens admitted in rule (b): the frame REPLACES the first person and nothing else — the modal contractions `i'd`, `i'll`, `we'd`, `we'll` are NOT in the set (their `would` / `will` is a polarity-bearing fact) and stay verbatim under either option (codex PR-MED-011); (b) frame tokens only — "Pt reports: I've had this pain in my lower back", the first person kept. Decide after: 0.1. Blocks: R.2. Done when: the practitioner's words are on this task and D2 carries the dated line. **DECIDED 2026-11-08 — (a) the swap. Against `3a42301`, the ONLY `own_voice`-specific change is D4's four words in the shorthand line ("introduce none beyond the Shorthand list"); everything else v3/v4 added (rule 1's Shorthand clause, rule 2's frame sentence and N/52 — about +40 words, roughly +60 tokens — and the per-section Shorthand / Speaker lines when they occur) is in BOTH styles. The fixtures' style profile is small (shorthand `HVLA`, `Cx`; two short exemplars; "third person, past tense", about 9 words a sentence), so the block is a few dozen tokens. Every call re-evaluates the whole prompt (`LocalLanguageModel.complete` clears the runtime's state after each call, PR-MED-034). (b) Growth per section: about +60 tokens of instruction in both styles, plus the Shorthand line (about 6–10 tokens) where a phrase matches and the Speaker line (about 5) where the section is patient-spoken — at Task 0.3's 57.6 prompt tokens/s that is about +1.1–1.3 s, which matches `own_voice`'s +1.20 s but NOT `narrative`'s −0.11 s on the same growth. So the prompt text alone does not explain the split; two hypotheses the runtime's own counts can settle: (1) the `own_voice` prompt crosses a runtime batch boundary under v3/v4 that the shorter `narrative` prompt does not (llama-cpp-python's default `n_batch` is 512 tokens; the app does not set it, `language_model.py:286-291`), or (2) the slow sections GENERATE more than their short prose shows (a stripped `<think>` block or a long completion cut by the parser). (a) The instruction-shaped line: `own_voice` is the only style whose system text carries a person line ("third person (no I or we; still no subject the lines do not contain)") and a style instruction ("Match the clinician's own writing style"), and v3/v4 placed a third-person subject in the instruction for the first time ("'Pt reports' or 'The patient reports'"); the line itself names "the patient", and the failing prose opens "Patient is cured." — the likeliest trigger is the person line meeting the new frame wording, but other conditioning elements are not ruled out. Options: (a) `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS` from 0.3's rates so one window with context runs about 30–45 s (the longest wait any exit path or the close guard can see), context lines on; (b) a larger slice (fewer system-prompt re-evaluations, longer waits), named; (c) context lines off (asked lines only) if 0.3's rates put a usable slice with context past the 240 s cap for a 25-minute consultation.   - **Executor derivation (stage-2 leg x1, 2026-11-09T04:44:25+11:00) — a DRAFT for the practitioner's decision (the MUST-PAUSE in the handoff note); nothing decided, D9 not yet amended.** Formula per window: seconds = prompt tokens / 57.6 + answer tokens / 11.4 (Task 0.3's means; the system prompt is re-evaluated on every window because `_clear_inference_state` drops the cache per call). Inputs, each an ESTIMATE and named as one: (1) the fixed part of every prompt ≈ 560 tokens — `SUGGEST_INSTRUCTION` is not written yet (C.1), so it is estimated from D7 / D9's specification at ≈ 500 (the task ≈ 60; the 17 keys of `note.CANONICAL_SECTIONS` with a one-line meaning and owner hint ≈ 300; the data-never-instructions sentence ≈ 30; the answer form ≈ 40), plus ≈ 20 for the chat template and ≈ 40 for `LINES BEGIN` / `LINES END` and the "Answer one line per `?` number" line; every 100 tokens more adds 1.7 s to every window; (2) segments: a transcript segment is one VAD speech span (`transcription.assemble_transcript`; split on `speech.MIN_SILENCE_SECONDS = 0.35`), and the one recorded rate is the Phase 2 benchmark — 19 segments in 81.9 s of the clinical script, 10 in 53.2 s of the non-clinical text (`plan-phase2-recording-transcription.md:875`; SAPI speech, not a consultation) — so 14 segments a minute, 350 for a 25-minute consultation, ≈ 10 words (≈ 13 tokens) a segment (189 words / 19); real consultations are unmeasured (C5: no real transcript is read); (3) line sizes: a context line `<n> <speaker> [placed: <key>] <text>` ≈ 28 tokens, an asked line `<n>? <speaker> <text>` ≈ 20 (Qwen splits digits; a key is ≈ 5 tokens); (4) left-out (asked) lines ≈ 30 % of the pre-boundary segments, ≈ 105 of 350 — an assumption, unmeasured on real consultations; (5) answer ≈ 8 tokens per asked line + 2 (D7's `max_tokens = 8 × asked + 16`); (6) every pre-boundary segment is in some window when context is on (a slice with no asked line is skipped — C.1's planner, rare at 30 %); the cap is checked BEFORE each window (D9), so a job can end up to one window past 240 s. Results (seconds of model time; the 25-minute line is the plan's question, the 10-minute line for scale):
    - **(a) context on, `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 40`:** a window ≈ (560 + 25 × 40) / 57.6 + (8 × 12 + 2) / 11.4 = 27.1 + 8.6 ≈ **36 s** (inside the 30–45 s target). 25 minutes: 9 windows needed (8 × 40 + 30), ≈ 315 s — PAST the 240 s cap; windows start at 0, 36, … 214 s, so 7 run and the job ends ≈ 250 s; 280 of 350 segments assessed, the last 70 (≈ 21 of the ≈ 105 left-out lines, the consultation's last ≈ 5 minutes) reported not assessed. Consultations up to ≈ 20 minutes (280 segments) are assessed whole. 10 minutes: 4 windows, ≈ 130 s, all assessed.
    - **(b) a larger slice, context on, `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 100`:** a window ≈ (560 + 2,500) / 57.6 + (8 × 30 + 2) / 11.4 = 53.1 + 21.2 ≈ **74 s** (past the 30–45 s target; the prompt 3,060 + `max_tokens` 256 + framing 64 = 3,380 of the 4,096-token context — the context allows about 125 segments at most, so 100 leaves room for longer lines before the D7 halving). 25 minutes: 4 windows (3 × 100 + 50), starting at 0, 74, 149, 223 s — all run, the job ends ≈ 265 s, every segment assessed; up to ≈ 28 minutes (400 segments) assessed whole. 10 minutes: 2 windows, ≈ 110 s.
    - **(c) context off (asked lines only), `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 25` asked lines:** a window ≈ (560 + 20 × 25) / 57.6 + (8 × 25 + 2) / 11.4 = 18.4 + 17.7 ≈ **36 s** (the answer dominates: every line in the window is asked). 25 minutes: 105 asked lines, 5 windows (4 × 25 + 5), ≈ 160 s, all assessed, well under the cap (the cap would bind only past ≈ 175 asked lines). 10 minutes: 2 windows, ≈ 64 s. The cost is routing quality: a short answer ("yeah, about three weeks") is sent without the question it answers unless that question was also left out.
    - **Common to all three:** (i) the result lands ONCE, when the job ends (C.1's one `SuggestionStageResult`; D8's landing), so no mark shows before then — ≈ 4 min 10 s / 4 min 25 s / 2 min 40 s for a 25-minute consultation under (a) / (b) / (c), plus ≈ 10 s for the model's load under Clean clinical (the default style: no prose job has loaded it; whether the load is inside the job's clock is C.1's, the clock starting at the first window), or after the prose job under a prose style (D8 arms the job on its release); (ii) the longest wait any path sees is ONE window — (a) ≈ 36 s, (b) ≈ 74 s, (c) ≈ 36 s — for the window-close guard, a Recovery-tab open-review of another recording, and Save after an Add (the new prose job waits behind the in-flight window, D8 residue (1)); the same figure is how long transcript text stays in memory after Complete or Discard (D8 residue (3)); Complete and Discard themselves never wait (abort and orphan, the key destroyed first — `main_window.py:2066-2078`); about 10 s more if the close lands during the model's first load; (iii) the laptop's speed drifted about 35 % between sessions in Phase R (Last Session): at 35 % slower, (a)'s window is ≈ 48 s and the cap assesses ≈ 14 of 25 minutes; (b)'s ≈ 100 s and ≈ 21 of 25 minutes; (c)'s ≈ 49 s and still all of them (≈ 215 s); (iv) Task 0.3 measured prompt evaluation at about 2,500 tokens — a shorter prompt evaluates somewhat faster per token, so the small windows' figures lean slow.
    - **Executor recommendation: (a) — `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 40`, context lines on.** It meets D9's 30–45 s target; what the cap cuts on a long consultation is its END (windows run in transcript order) — mostly the clinician's own treatment, advice and plan lines, class W, which already carry a left-out-line warning — while the early history, where the silent class U lives, is always assessed; and the context is what lets a short answer be routed at all (D9's Why). Stated plainly: by this estimate a full 25-minute consultation with context needs ≈ 315 s, so D9's trigger for (c) ("if context doubles the time past the cap") is met on paper — (c) is a fair choice if the practitioner would rather every line be looked at than the short answers be routed well. C.6's seen sets are short scripts that fit in one window under every option, so run 9 measures (a) against (c) only through the context choice.

the job's `yield_to` waits while `_prose_pending` is set or until the abort (`abort.wait(0.05)` — an exit wakes it at once, a prose release is seen within 50 ms; `_SUGGESTION_YIELD_POLL_SECONDS`); the holder pattern (the closure keeps no reference to the input); landing (`_on_suggest_done`) keeps ids and keys only (`_suggested`), dropped when orphaned; a raised job names its exception TYPE only (`models.SUGGESTION_FAILED_LINE`; its message could carry an input value — narrower than the prose stage's line). The fix: `NoteScreen(..., suggestion_model_present: Callable[[], bool] | None = None)` — the window passes its `_language_model_available` seam; `_begin_suggestions` asks it ON THE GUI THREAD after the nothing-to-suggest check (the stage's own order) and, when absent, names `suggestion_model_absent_line()` and starts NO thread. So every existing window test (all built through `_main_window`, whose seam says absent, or the default seam over the conftest's empty models root) runs exactly as before C.4. Presence stays a stat asked per review, never kept (round 20 MED-002); the stage still asks it again on its worker (unchanged). No existing `is_busy` / close test needed changing. (3) Measurement, `confirm-all --prose`: `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/run-validation.py C:/ncd-c6/<set> --config validation/config --rule validation/rules/rule-v5.json --set <set> --suggestions confirm-all --prose < /dev/null > .cursor/loops/stage-2-c6-confirm-<set>.md; echo "exit=$?"` — an ESTIMATE of (2)'s time plus about 40–60 s an encounter (about three prose sections at 6–7.5 s each, R.5's means; one suggestion window, ≤ C.0's ≈ 36 s since the seen scripts are short; one model load of about 10 s a run): about +35–50 min for the 50 and +10–20 min for each held-out set, so roughly 2.5–7 h for the six. Record on this task, per set: the exit code; the four verdict lines; the TOTAL row whole (its six suggestion totals — Suggested W, Suggested U, Suggested dropped, Lines not assessed, Wrongly present via suggestion, Model seconds); the summed `ProseCounts` (passed / failed / errored, as R.6 summed them — the TOTAL prose cell is `-`); every encounter with `Wrongly present via suggestion` > 0, with `Lines not assessed` > 0 or with an `unavailable` cell (ids and counts); every encounter whose Rule cell differs from (2)'s (ids and the failure codes); and both reports' SHA-256. The reports stay git-ignored under `.cursor/loops/`; afterwards delete `C:\ncd-c6\` (synthetic only). These are MEASUREMENTS for predictions v6 (F.1, re-run at the post-hardening commit if a later fix changes behaviour — D11, C6), never the run of record.

  - **Diagnosis of the new R3 counts and the wrongly-present facts — (ii) NOT A DEFECT, a measured cost of `confirm-all` (2026-11-09T11:15:01+11:00, leg x8; ids, segment roles and counts only, from the composer's synthetic reports and the code; no `validation/heldout-*` file read).** The NEW R3s are h3-13 (`heldout-3`; h3-17 was R3 under `off` already) and h5-12 (`heldout-5`; h5-10 likewise). In each, the next patient's absent fact (h3-13/f4, h5-12/f5) moves correctly_absent → wrongly_present and is attributed to a suggested line (D10's inclusive rule, `validation.py:2292-2303`); h5-12's ONE addition is the other speaker's line (W 0, U 1), so that line carried it; h3-13 added 7 W and 3 U and the report, counts only by design, does not name which (R3's own term places the carrying statement in the reference tail, `fact.line >= reference.tail_start`, `validation.py:2282`; tails 10 and 16, `:680`, `:703`). Why it is not a defect: R3 holds only when the run's finalised note has NO next-patient boundary (`boundary is None`, `:2283`, read from the note's Check 6 warning by `next_patient_segment`, `:2060`) and the script's own words have none either (`reference.boundary is None`, `:2284`; the reports' reference-boundary line: h3-13 none / 10, h5-12 none / 16); neither note carries `possible_next_patient` (the checker tallies). With no boundary, `eligible_utterances` has nothing to refuse (`ui/models.py:3010-3013` refuses a suggestion only when `boundary is not None and index >= boundary`) — in the harness (`validation.py:3237`, `note_config.next_patient_boundary(document, inputs.config)`) and in the Note tab (`ui/note.py:1466-1468`, `next_patient_segment(note.note_warnings)`) alike, because Check 6's warning is built from that same accessor (`note_check.py:2079-2088`) on the same config (the harness finalises with `inputs.config`); the suggestion input is cut at the same boundary (`build_note_request`, `validation.py:3219-3221`). No encounter WITH a Check 6 boundary has a fact wrongly present via suggestion (e.g. h3-01, h3-02, h3-12: 0). So the suggestion reaches exactly the residue R3 names — an unbroken next patient, bounded by a ceiling — where under `off` the provider happened to leave the tail out. h3-18 (wrongly present via suggestion, NOT R3): the script's words carry a boundary (8, tail 9 — one of the report's listed mismatches) but the run's transcript does not (no `possible_next_patient`): the recogniser broke the boundary phrase, and R3 excuses only a next patient even the script's words cannot find (`:2284`), so it counts as material_wrong — h3-18 PASS → FAIL; the same mechanism, not a defect; its carrying line (of 6 W, 3 U) is not named in the report. Also measured, outside the six columns (D10 counts `wrongly_present` only): two material facts moved omitted → wrong — a misheard line left out under `off` entered through a suggested line (the additions are the only difference between the policies: prose yields counts only and the fact walk reads the finalised note, `validation.py:3371-3387`): h2-15/f9 (not R1, so h2-15 PASS → FAIL material_wrong; h2-15's four additions are the clinician's) and h3-13/f2 (a marked R1 — under `heldout-3`'s ceiling, R1 2 over 1; its unsupported line excused by the R1 exemption). No pin added and no `--only` rerun owed (nothing was fixed). These are the inputs predictions v6 (F.1) carry, and P.6's register rows ("a row per `Wrongly present via suggestion`"); D11 already rejects a predicate change for suggestions, so nothing here reopens a locked decision — the practitioner's reading is the non-blocking note in the handoff.

Decided by the practitioner on 2026-11-10, "Fix .gitattributes, push (Recommended)": one line, `validation/rules/*.json text eol=lf`, with no code, rule or test change (C6 holds). That commit becomes run 9's run-of-record commit and H5's build commit, in place of `38af5c2`.

*To resume in a new session: open a fresh Agent (Ctrl+I), run /start-session, then run /load-plan*
*Sections marked (Only if relevant) can be omitted when genuinely not applicable, but never omit decisions, constraints, verification steps, or integration knowledge that a fresh session would otherwise have to rediscover.*
*For lightweight plans, plans with fewer than 5 tasks, or plans created without a preceding `/explore` discussion, sections with no real content should be marked `N/A — simple feature` rather than padded with placeholder structure.*
*State-once convention (new plans): state each cross-section contract — a critical constraint, design decision, invariant, or other normative rule — once in its canonical section, and have every other section point to it rather than restate it. Scope guard: this governs cross-section normative duplication only — the Task Specification Standard (in `/create-plan` and `/review-plan`) and task-local detail are untouched, and a plan recording `fast/medium involved` in its Executor tier may keep extra restatement where it helps the fast executor. It applies to new plans as they are written; never retroactively rewrite an existing plan to conform.*
